> evm.asm, our collaboration with EF on an e2e verified EVM implementation for zkVMs, now has its own autoresearch competition: https://evm.fast
>
> Prove the lowest runtime to validate an Ethereum block! https://twitter.com/ethereumfndn/status/2117474349792006258

**Links in post:** https://evm.fast, https://twitter.com/ethereumfndn/status/2117474349792006258

**Quoted post (full text):**

    #### Quoted post — @1529875152460193794 — 2026-11-03 04:30 UTC (2026-11-03 05:30 WAT)
    
    - URL: https://x.com/1529875152460193794/status/2117474349792006258
    - Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 30, impression 26546, like 177, quote 11, reply 10, repost 23
    
    > What's the most work that could possibly be required to verify an Ethereum block?
    >
    > Today, we can't prove an answer. All we know is the validator always finishes. http://evm.fast is an open challenge to change that: contributors compete on a leaderboard to find mathematically guaranteed bounds for verifying Ethereum, no matter what's in a block.
    >
    > Built on Yukon in collaboration with @zksecurityXYZ, @Veritylab, and @yukonresearch, http://evm.fast seeks provable limits that keep verification cheap, predictable, and possible on modest hardware.
    >
    > Try it yourself:
    > https://evm.fast
    
    **Links in post:** https://evm.fast, http://evm.fast

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2115190671673847958
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 2, impression 1004, like 12, quote 0, reply 0, retweet 4

> if you guys used https://t.co/bvzYDn3258 it would detect weak keys automatically, just saying https://t.co/po9MOFu1Qd

**Quoted post (full text):**

    #### Quoted post — @128391252 — 2026-10-26 20:51 UTC (2026-10-26 21:51 WAT)
    
    - URL: https://x.com/128391252/status/2114822201555943763
    - Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 2747, impression 1160090, like 3753, quote 460, reply 161, retweet 592
    
    > Announcing 3 Critical vulns for hundreds of millions of IoT, Firewalls, OT, and Military devices including: Palo Alto Networks, Comcast, D-Link, HP, Canon, Honeywell, GE, Oracle, Siemens, Rockwell, Digi, Lockheed Martin, Boeing... 
    >
    > The vulns are with GoAhead Web Server which is a backend library used by these devices. There's a patch available from GoAhead but that doesn't mean there's a patch available for the devices yet -- that's up to the manufacturer.
    >
    > - TLS traffic over DHE ciphers have a 1-byte secret, "04", meaning anyone can decrypt connections to these devices and steal login credentials and session tokens if the client negotiated with DHE ciphers like the latest TLS_DHE_RSA_WITH_CHACHA20_POLY1305_SHA256, which is extremely likely.
    >
    > - LibreSSL mTLS has been completely bypassed, meaning anyone can connect over mTLS. If you have one of these devices listening on the internet and are expecting mTLS to protect it from attackers, take it offline or patch asap.
    >
    > - OpenSSL Certificate Revocation Lists (CRLs) are not enforced, meaning if a user device is stolen or compromised, and you revoke that mTLS cert, nothing actually happens. There are a lot of other attacks this can be used for.
    >
    > These are critical because these types of devices run our world. Water systems, electricity, military, firewalls, critical infrastructure.
    >
    > https://foxio.io/blog/choose-your-backend-three-tls-bugs-in-goahead
    
    **Links in post:** https://foxio.io/blog/choose-your-backend-three-tls-bugs-in-goahead

## 2026-10-25 · Thread · New https://zk.golf challenge ⛳️ Google ID Token Claims Extraction.


Tags (automatic): ZK, AI-assisted hunting


### Post — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-10-25 09:14 UTC (2026-10-25 10:14 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2114284396450893976
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 9, impression 1833, like 27, quote 0, reply 3, retweet 4

> New https://zk.golf challenge ⛳️ Google ID Token Claims Extraction.
>
> Take the raw JSON payload of a Google ID token, parse it, check it against Google's schema, and extract the claim values. All inside a circuit. (1/8) [pic.x.com/0BStN6wHnc]

**Media:**
- photo: https://pbs.twimg.com/media/HVdxRE6WIAAPFzs.jpg — no alt text provided by author

**Links in post:** https://zk.golf


### Thread part 2 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-10-25 09:14 UTC (2026-10-25 10:14 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2114284399906652277
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 236, like 1, quote 0, reply 2, retweet 0

> Parsing JSON is a fundamental primitive for verifying JWTs in zk circuits.
> Checking the signature isn't enough: the circuit has to extract the values it cares about (iss, aud, email, exp…) so that it can enforce properties on them. (2/8)


### Thread part 3 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-10-25 09:14 UTC (2026-10-25 10:14 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2114284401819279837
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 149, like 1, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> JSON parsing in a circuit has always been hard. The circuit has to handle a lot of variation: extra fields it doesn't expect, nested objects, escapes, and keys that can appear in any order. (3/8)


### Thread part 4 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-10-25 09:14 UTC (2026-10-25 10:14 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2114284404977844444
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 43, like 1, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> A lot of existing circuits take shortcuts: "it's signed, so it must look the way Google writes it", and they skip full JSON parsing.
> A common trick is substring extraction: find the key, then read up to the next quote. (4/8)


### Thread part 5 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-10-25 09:14 UTC (2026-10-25 10:14 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2114284407276114266
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 48, like 1, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> This challenge takes no shortcuts.
> The circuit gets the raw bytes and must accept exactly the valid tokens. Every accepted input must be:
> - a valid JSON document
> - matching the ID token schema we defined in the spec
> - with each extracted key appearing at most once in the document (5/8)


### Thread part 6 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-10-25 09:14 UTC (2026-10-25 10:14 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2114284410425991177
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 44, like 1, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> Here’s an example extraction. The set of claims to extract is fixed: for each schema key present in the input document, the “presence” flag is set to 1 and the value is extracted. Missing keys get a 0 flag and zeroed slots, and any extra keys in the document are ignored. (6/8) [pic.x.com/GUFtM77fWT]

**Media:**
- photo: https://pbs.twimg.com/media/HVdyAolWgAATazx.jpg — no alt text provided by author


### Thread part 7 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-10-25 09:14 UTC (2026-10-25 10:14 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2114284413311655981
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 138, like 1, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> The best part: we never wrote the circuit ourselves.
> We wrote the specification: a reference JSON parser and a simple declarative schema of what an acceptable Google ID token looks like. A few days later, an agent had written the corresponding circuit. (7/8)


### Thread part 8 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-10-25 09:14 UTC (2026-10-25 10:14 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2114284416633884845
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 1, impression 121, like 1, quote 0, reply 0, retweet 0

> Think you can implement it using fewer constraints?
> Prove it! ⛳️
> https://zk.golf (8/8)

**Quoted post (full text):**

    #### Quoted post — @2312333412 — 2026-09-03 12:18 UTC (2026-09-03 13:18 WAT)
    
    - URL: https://x.com/2312333412/status/2095486647634645071
    - Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 304, impression 496953, like 2821, quote 148, reply 691, retweet 542
    
    > Ethereum is for shipping.
    >
    > Here are 35 things the Ethereum ecosystem launched, upgraded, and announced through August.
    >
    > 1/ GnosisDAO approved a vote to transition @gnosischain from its own L1 to a ZK-proven Ethereum L2 rollup with synchronous composability, so apps on Gnosis and Ethereum can interact in a single transaction.
    >
    > 2/ @BlackRock introduced the BlackRock Select Treasury Based Liquidity Fund (BSTBL) with a tokenized share class on Ethereum mainnet for managing stablecoin and digital asset reserves, and began tokenizing share classes of its $311B European money market fund series on Ethereum.
    >
    > 3/ @aztecnetwork launched Alpha v5, a protocol upgrade that reduced private transaction proving times and brought the first wave of privacy-preserving apps to the network.
    >
    > 4/ @Uniswap processed $1B+ in stock token volume and $20B+ in total volume on @RobinhoodCrypto's Robinhood Chain since its July launch.
    >
    > 5/ @ethPandaOps introduced the Platåberget testnet, preparing client implementations for Glamsterdam, Ethereum's next network upgrade.
    >
    > 6/ @Morpho crossed $880M in total deposits on Robinhood Chain in its first <2 months live and reached $5.75B in total deposits on @base.
    >
    > 7/ @web3privacy shared the Ethereum Privacy Ecosystem Mapping 2026, an updated map and repository covering Ethereum’s privacy ecosystem.
    >
    > 8/ @Revolut announced the phased rollout of its euro-denominated stablecoin EURR, live on Ethereum for eligible users.
    >
    > 9/ @aave v4 surpassed $525M in deposits on Ethereum.
    >
    > 10/ Coinbase Tokenized Stocks launched on @base for non-U.S. users, held 1:1 by a regulated custodian, owned in self-custody wallets, and usable across DeFi 24/7.
    >
    > 11/ @FreedomFactory opened presales for the PQ1, a fully open-source, air-gapped hardware wallet that signs with post-quantum cryptography through an Ethereum smart account.
    >
    > 12/ @OctantApp launched Epoch 13, its latest funding round, focused on privacy on Ethereum and across the open internet.
    >
    > 13/ @selfxyz and Google Cloud launched the USAT Mainnet Faucet on @Celo, letting anyone verified on Self claim USA₮.
    >
    > 14/ @PrivacyBoost launched the Privacy Boost App, a frontend to send private transfers from your connected wallet.
    >
    > 15/ @Whitechain_io, the distribution-focused network connected to the WhiteBit ecosystem, announced it is evolving from an L1 into an Ethereum L2 built on the OP Stack.
    >
    > 16/ @ether_fi added tokenized stocks, portfolio-backed loans through @aave, and other new features to its crypto neobank.
    >
    > 17/ @ethereuminsti announced its ecosystem funding round and supporter coalition, welcoming 100+ organizations to support Ethereum's institutional onboarding, and shared that Open Standard's Open USD stablecoin will launch on Ethereum on day one.
    >
    > 18/ @class_lambda and @gattacahq shared that their PropAMM (pAMMs) infrastructure has facilitated over $2B in total volume swapped on Ethereum.
    >
    > 19/ @theInterfold, an open-source protocol for confidential coordination and encrypted execution environments built on Ethereum, launched its Network Alpha production environment.
    >
    > 20/ @arbitrum activated ArbOS Elara, bringing more responsive fees and 4x bigger Stylus smart contracts to Arbitrum One, along with new features for chains built on the Arbitrum stack.
    >
    > 21/ @Uniswap launched v4 Permissioned Pools, a hook standard to enforce allowlists on swaps and liquidity deposits while the protocol itself stays permissionless, bringing regulated assets to AMM trading with launch partners @SuperstateInc, @Securitize, and Dowgo.
    >
    > 22/ @EthCoordinate, a new crypto-native organization building on the EthStaker community's work, was formed to support Ethereum governance coordination, facilitate stakeholder interaction, and accelerate Ethereum adoption.
    >
    > 23/ @MetaMask launched the MetaMask Agent Wallet, an agentic wallet with built-in spending limits, allowlists, and risk profiles.
    >
    > 24/ @ResearchHub, a tool for the open funding, publication, and discussion of scientific research built on Ethereum and @base, launched a new homepage to surface open research proposals and funding requests.
    >
    > 25/ @base opened applications for Base Batches 004, a startup accelerator program for 10 early-stage teams, and also launched Base Verify Onchain, a privacy-preserving identity layer for apps to enforce one-person-one-claim onchain distributions.
    >
    > 26/ @Cloudflare announced Wallets, enabling AI agents to buy APIs and content with stablecoin payments over the x402 payment standard.
    >
    > 27/ The @ethereumfndn launched the better[.]codes autoresearch challenge to advance post-quantum Ethereum, built with @zksecurityXYZ and @eigenlabs on @yukonresearch, putting a machine-verified security problem on a public leaderboard that anyone can push forward. @Lighter_xyz also launched the Lighter Prover challenge, an autoresearch competition to make Lighter's production exchange faster.
    >
    > 28/ Privacy-focused wallet @staycloakedxyz reached $650k in deposits and $1M in volume in its first 90 days live.
    >
    > 29/ @mtpelerin added support for @ensdomains names as sending and receiving addresses when creating personal IBANs for bank transfers in crypto.
    >
    > 30/ @Blockspace_ETH was announced, a company focused on out-of-protocol infrastructure for Ethereum blockspace.
    >
    > 31/ @0xprivacypools launched onchain payroll support, allowing employers to provide recurring wage payments with privacy for salary amounts and recipient addresses.
    >
    > 32/ Fake World Assets (FWA), a NFT gacha protocol by @token_works, launched the FWAir NFT distribution mechanism with an initial 111 item PFP collection.
    >
    > 33/ Builder @z0r0zzz launched zSwap, a fully onchain decentralized exchange built on Ethereum, with all code, including frontend and application logic, stored on mainnet smart contracts.
    >

    > We’re happy to support the proximity prize challenge efforts, now with machine-verifiability! 
    >
    > http://better.codes currently hosts two challenges, to improve what we know about the security bounds of proximity gaps. Since the challenges are expressed in Lean, every accepted result carries a strong guarantee, in the same style as http://zk.golf.

> We’re happy to support the proximity prize challenge efforts, now with machine-verifiability! 
>
> http://better.codes currently hosts two challenges, to improve what we know about the security bounds of proximity gaps. Since the challenges are expressed in Lean, every accepted result carries a strong guarantee, in the same style as http://zk.golf.
>
> This follows the series of efforts in the industry and in our team to utilize verifiability for fearless optimization.
>
> Starting from a security bits baseline of 53 (lower bound) and 128 (upper bound), AIs (and people too) will collaborate to improve the state of the art, and hopefully interesting ideas that can advance science will rise out of it!

> * Executors: every zkVM side by side either via Ere

- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 1, impression 134, like 2, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> So we built cheatVM: a cheatcode-enabled execution harness for Aleo, roughly Foundry for the Aleo ecosystem.
>
> Deploy, fund accounts, set mappings, execute, assert finalization. One Rust test, end to end.
>
> Readable for humans, and usable by the agent to triage itself.


### Thread part 2 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-07-20 13:15 UTC (2026-07-20 14:15 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2079193415753822433
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 1, impression 82, like 2, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> Third lesson: no threat model, no signal.
>
> 14 false positives out of 33 findings. Eight of them were simply not in the threat model.
>
> We gave zkao a zkao.md with scope, trust boundaries, and severity preferences. False positives dropped to 2.


### Thread part 3 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-07-20 13:15 UTC (2026-07-20 14:15 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2079193417137918066
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 1, impression 185, like 2, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> The through line: vibe auditing alone is not enough.
>
> An LLM is a real accelerant, but it still needs a domain expert to give it knowledge, give it tools that produce actual proof, and tell it what counts as an attack.

> We just brought Leo and Aleo support into zkao, our AI bug-detection tool, and put it to work on a real engagement with HumanityLink.
>
> The hard part was not the model. It was everything we had to build around it.


### Thread part 2 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-07-20 13:15 UTC (2026-07-20 14:15 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2079193409718218765
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 2, impression 357, like 3, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> First lesson: a strong model with no ecosystem knowledge misses trivial bugs.
>
> HumanityLink's Leo contract had a custom constructor with no ownership check. Anyone could upgrade the program.
>
> zkao missed it. So did vanilla Claude and ChatGPT.


### Thread part 3 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-07-20 13:15 UTC (2026-07-20 14:15 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2079193411257610625
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 2, impression 262, like 3, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> We found it by hand, then taught it back to the agent.
>
> Four knowledge components: a Leo security cheat sheet, an Aleo Instructions reference, snarkVM design gotchas, and a catalog of Aleo/Leo bug patterns.
>
> It catches the class consistently now.

**Quoted post (full text):**

    #### Quoted post — @1996086952970649605 — 2026-07-10 02:01 UTC (2026-07-10 03:01 WAT)
    
    - URL: https://x.com/1996086952970649605/status/2075400032937775326
    - Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 10, impression 7187, like 35, quote 1, reply 2, retweet 6
    
    > https://t.co/BrmPhwddMm by @zksecurityXYZ challenges you to write the cheapest formally verified circuit and prove it correct in Lean 4.
    >
    > For the SHA-256 challenge, we submitted a circuit with 1 witness and 1 constraint. It still verified.
    >
    > Here is how we broke the spec. 🧵 https://t.co/2B4OFOA4co
    
    **Media:**
    - media `3_2075400030249267200` (details not returned by the API)
    


### Thread part 2 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-07-13 12:02 UTC (2026-07-13 13:02 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2076638352086823081
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 349, like 1, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> In Clean circuits, when defining a witness generator you can reference other variables. By being self-referential, you could make our completeness statement vacuous, because one of the hypotheses becomes contradictory from assuming a witness that is not constructible.


### Thread part 3 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-07-13 12:02 UTC (2026-07-13 13:02 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2076638353940750495
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 305, like 0, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> What was missing is a property that enforces computability of witnesses, by requiring a strict dependency order. When adding such a property, Clean's completeness is strengthened to the proper statement: That there exists a witness that satisfies the constraints.


### Thread part 4 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-07-13 12:02 UTC (2026-07-13 13:02 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2076638355840712992
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 153, like 0, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> We updated https://t.co/o878hqHbsh to require the property as an additional proof. This closes the gap @QED_Audit exploited.
> Meanwhile, we're working on wiring the property natively into our framework https://t.co/cLq3J3BSTY
> The necessary theory has long existed in Clean..


### Thread part 5 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-07-13 12:02 UTC (2026-07-13 13:02 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2076638357740744821
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 120, like 0, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> ..but so far, we didn't take its importance seriously enough because we hadn't operated under the threat model of "adversarially written circuits".
> We think it's quite powerful to operate in this model, though, and happy to see zkGolf stress-test it for the first time.


### Thread part 6 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-07-13 12:02 UTC (2026-07-13 13:02 WAT)

- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 114, like 2, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

## 2026-07-03 · Post · RSA: `default` is the all-zero modulus, which isn't a valid key, so it fails the circuit's precondit


Tags (automatic): ZK


### Post — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-07-03 17:43 UTC (2026-07-03 18:43 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2073100430775668997
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 203, like 1, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> RSA: `default` is the all-zero modulus, which isn't a valid key, so it fails the circuit's precondition: A -&gt; B is true, when A is false...
>
> The default branch returns an empty circuit and passes:
>
> if input == default:
>     return nothing
> else:
>     run HonestCircuit

> Nice one!

> Someone found a bug in how we measure "cost" on https://t.co/o878hqHbsh. Not in Clean/Lean, the circuits are sound, but our scoring was off... let's see how. 🧵 https://t.co/DhTepO42Ll

### Thread part 2 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-07-03 17:43 UTC (2026-07-03 18:43 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2073100424945479839
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 1, impression 484, like 5, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> To score a circuit we ran it on one fixed input, `default` and we checked correctness on a a symbolic all-variable input; so it is correct for every input.
>
> So cost was looking at one input and correctness/completeness at every input. 
>
> That's the bug in essence.

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2072701384416206988
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 771, like 8, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> Rules: the cheapest zk circuit for the challenge is king of the hill. 👑
> Every submission must ship a Lean proof that it's sound and complete against the spec.
> If Lean is happy, we are happy.


### Thread part 4 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-07-02 15:18 UTC (2026-07-02 16:18 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2072701386706305208
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 1, impression 845, like 13, quote 0, reply 2, retweet 1

> How we got here: we wrote a Lean spec for SHA-256. 
> Then we asked an LLM to write an optimized circuit.
>
> The result was the most efficient SHA-256 compression in R1CS by a wide margin.
> We never reviewed the circuit, it literally cannot be broken.
>
> We call it fearless optimization


### Thread part 5 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-07-02 15:18 UTC (2026-07-02 16:18 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2072701388740612353
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 626, like 8, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> It works because of one asymmetry: 
> The specs can be simple but the circuits implementing them can be complex. LLMs are happy to do the boring work of proving that the circuit matches the spec. Humans only need to read the spec.
> Sometimes you can trust the magic talking box!

## 2026-05-26 · Thread · On May 15, THORChain was drained for ~$10.7M. Root cause: a flawed implementation of GG20, an MPC-ba


Tags (automatic): ZK


### Post — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-05-26 04:15 UTC (2026-05-26 05:15 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2059126128510398752
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 27, impression 5420, like 61, quote 0, reply 4, retweet 12

> On May 15, THORChain was drained for ~$10.7M. Root cause: a flawed implementation of GG20, an MPC-based threshold signature scheme.
> Theoretically sound MPC schemes keep failing in implementation. So we built a reference for it 👇


### Thread part 2 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-05-26 04:15 UTC (2026-05-26 05:15 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2059126131157045647
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 12, impression 4130, like 33, quote 1, reply 2, retweet 12

> Today we're launching https://t.co/25RLaELMX4, a collaborative catalog of common pitfalls in MPC implementations and the cryptographic primitives they rely on.
> Built with contributors from zkSecurity, Trail of Bits, Partisia, and Zama.


### Thread part 3 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-05-26 04:15 UTC (2026-05-26 05:15 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2059126132746674251
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 2, impression 578, like 4, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 1

> The idea came out of RWMPC 2025. A group of practitioners agreed the field needed a shared, living reference for the implementation mistakes that keep recurring.
> https://t.co/25RLaELMX4 is for the gap between "provably secure on paper" and "shipping in production."


### Thread part 4 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-05-26 04:15 UTC (2026-05-26 05:15 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2059126134155931695
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 290, like 2, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> The guide currently covers six categories:
>
> - Input validation
> - Context binding
> - Concurrency &amp; state
> - Insecure subprotocols
> - Failure recovery
> - Adaptive inputs
>
> Plus misuse of the cryptographic primitives MPC protocols depend on.

## 2026-02-27 · Thread · The first two known exploits against live ZK circuits just happened, and they weren't subtle underco


Tags (automatic): Blockchain / L1-L2 / bridge, ZK, AI-assisted hunting


### Post — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-02-27 16:15 UTC (2026-02-27 17:15 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2027417243059896544
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 191, impression 53116, like 373, quote 20, reply 17, retweet 83

> The first two known exploits against live ZK circuits just happened, and they weren't subtle underconstrained bugs. 
>
> They were Groth16 verifiers deployed without completing the trusted setup ceremony. One was  white-hat rescued for ~$1.5M, the other drained for 5 ETH.  
>
> 🧵


### Thread part 2 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-02-27 16:15 UTC (2026-02-27 17:15 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2027417244590817733
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 2, impression 3655, like 32, quote 1, reply 1, retweet 2

> Both protocols used Circom + snarkjs, the most common stack for Groth16 deployments. 
>
> The bug? They skipped Phase 2 of the trusted setup: the circuit-specific contribution step.
>
> Without it, the verification key's γ and δ parameters are both set to the same value: the G2 gens


### Thread part 3 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-02-27 16:15 UTC (2026-02-27 17:15 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2027417252891345323
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 5, impression 2108, like 19, quote 1, reply 1, retweet 2

> The scary part: this wasn't a deep cryptographic flaw or a subtle circuit bug. It was a deployment mistake: missing one CLI command. And it sat in production with real funds at risk.


### Thread part 4 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-02-27 16:15 UTC (2026-02-27 17:15 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2027417255127003400
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 2, impression 2134, like 17, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 1

> takeaways:
> - Always review deployment scripts, not just circuit code
> - Check your verification keys: if vk_gamma_2 == vk_delta_2, your proofs are forgeable
> - We worked with @DedaubSecurity to scan EVM chains for other affected contracts, no high-value ones found, but some exist


### Thread part 5 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-02-27 16:15 UTC (2026-02-27 17:15 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2027417256762687769
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 9, impression 2016, like 30, quote 0, reply 0, retweet 1

> We've also added detection for this class of vulnerability to zkao, our AI-powered continuous security scanner for ZK circuits. A missing Phase 2 contribution is exactly the kind of deployment-level issue that benefits from automated checks.
>
> full post: https://blog.zksecurity.xyz/posts/groth16-setup-exploit/

**Links in post:** https://blog.zksecurity.xyz/posts/groth16-setup-exploit/

## 2026-02-06 · Thread · AI vulnerability research is non-deterministic.


Tags (automatic): ZK, Memory corruption / native, AI-assisted hunting


### Post — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-02-06 15:48 UTC (2026-02-06 16:48 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2019800264425848842
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 24, impression 6726, like 55, quote 3, reply 3, retweet 14

> AI vulnerability research is non-deterministic.
>
> The same model looking at the same code won't take the same reasoning path every time. Some paths find nothing. Some paths find the bug.
>
> A single scan is a coin flip. But what if you keep flipping?
>
> Introducing zkao. [pic.x.com/9KwUw3gpIn]

**Media:**
- photo: https://pbs.twimg.com/media/HAfFDhdXMAA3NPQ.jpg — no alt text provided by author


### Thread part 2 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-02-06 15:48 UTC (2026-02-06 16:48 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2019800266548060204
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 1, impression 565, like 4, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> @seanhn showed this clearly: using o3 on Linux kernel SMB code, the model found the vulnerability in 8 out of 100 runs. In many runs it concluded there was no bug.
>
> The punchline: if you keep running it, you stop needing luck.
>
> https://sean.heelan.io/2025/05/22/how-i-used-o3-to-find-cve-2025-37899-a-remote-zeroday-vulnerability-in-the-linux-kernels-smb-implementation/

**Links in post:** https://sean.heelan.io/2025/05/22/how-i-used-o3-to-find-cve-2025-37899-a-remote-zeroday-vulnerability-in-the-linux-kernels-smb-implementation/


### Thread part 3 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-02-06 15:48 UTC (2026-02-06 16:48 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2019800268313862592
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 459, like 3, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> zkao makes AI security research work the way fuzzing works: not as a one-shot event, but as something you run continuously until coverage compounds.
>
> We're starting with Circom, where we have deep expertise from 100+ ZK system audits.


### Thread part 4 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-02-06 15:48 UTC (2026-02-06 16:48 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2019800271233089795
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 274, like 2, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

> Your coverage improves along three axes:
>
> 1. models get better
> 2. our patterns get richer (learned from real Circom audits)
> 3. probability compounds with repeated runs
>
> Even if your code doesn't change, your security coverage does.


### Thread part 5 — @zksecurityXYZ — 2026-02-06 15:48 UTC (2026-02-06 16:48 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/2019800274190180540
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 0, impression 449, like 2, quote 0, reply 1, retweet 0

## 2025-07-22 · Thread · We reported this bug to a number of projects (including semaphore) after finding it with our interna


Tags (automatic): ZK, AI-assisted hunting


### Post — @zksecurityXYZ — 2025-07-22 12:55 UTC (2025-07-22 13:55 WAT)

- URL: https://x.com/zksecurityXYZ/status/1947641591327236590
- Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 15, impression 5682, like 49, quote 1, reply 2, retweet 11

> We reported this bug to a number of projects (including semaphore) after finding it with our internal AI tooling (SnarkSentinel). The bug would have allowed proving that arbitrary leaves were part of a Merkle tree! Read more about how SnarkSentinel helps us find bugs 👇🏼 https://twitter.com/PrivacyEthereum/status/1947407025694249302

**Links in post:** https://twitter.com/PrivacyEthereum/status/1947407025694249302

**Quoted post (full text):**

    #### Quoted post — @1409820375706394628 — 2025-07-21 21:23 UTC (2025-07-21 22:23 WAT)
    
    - URL: https://x.com/1409820375706394628/status/1947407025694249302
    - Metrics (at fetch): bookmark 25, impression 18110, like 117, quote 8, reply 10, retweet 22
    
    > Public Announcement: 
    >
    > 🚨 A bug was found in the BinaryMerkleRoot circuit in ZK-Kit. It allowed invalid Merkle tree leaves to generate valid ZK proofs.  
    >
    > This has been fixed in v2.0.0 of BinaryMerkleRoot.  
    >
    > @SemaphoreDevs v4, which relies on this circuit, is being updated accordingly. 
    >
    > All downstream projects using this ZK-Kit circuit and Semaphore v4 were notified in advance.  
    >
    > A new Trusted Setup Ceremony is coming soon. Stay tuned!  
    >
    > Details: https://pse.dev/blog/under-constrained-bug-in-binary-merkle-root-circuit-fixed-in-v200
    
    **Links in post:** https://pse.dev/blog/under-constrained-bug-in-binary-merkle-root-circuit-fixed-in-v200
